#2832. 2026-27 NBA Minutes Report: Injuries to Lauri Markkanen, Stephen Curry impact Western Conference landscape

Welcome to the NBA Minutes Report. Every week, I will review each team's updated minutes per game to see which players are seeing the court more or less than in previous weeks and what their impact has been. That can help you with any fantasy basketball decisions you may need to make, but also help you get a lay of the land on what's happening around the NBA and which players are gaining or losing value.

The charts below are also great for exploring on your own. You can track the minutes over the last three, five, ten games, and for the entire season, to see which trends stand out to you. All of this data was made accessible by Kyle Bland, who is incredibly talented and also extremely generous. Be sure to follow him to check out all of his baseball data as well.

Not much has changed for the Hawks since the start of the season. Offseason additions Lu Dort and Aaron Wiggins are playing key minutes in the rotation, and rookie Kingston Flemings has seen his minutes tick up as he's moved into the starting lineup over CJ McCollum. McCollum is still producing as a primary scorer off the bench, but his overall minutes and numbers have been down since the change.

The Celtics' rotation has been unchanged so far this season. Same as last week, Neemias Queta is playing far more minutes than newly-signed Mitchell Robinson. Robinson's fit in Joe Mazzulla's system seems tenuous right now. His rebounding numbers are down, particularly on the offensive glass where he is so dangerous, and the Celtics have been far better with him off the court. It's a situation they'll need to figure out.

Yes, that is Danny Wolf leading the Nets in minutes of late. He has moved into the starting lineup and is averaging 10.6 points, 7.0 rebounds, and 2.4 assists over his last five games. He and Day'Ron Sharpe have been good together, giving the Nets a clear rebounding edge. We should expect to see the two of them get a decent amount of minutes. We've also seen Egor Demin return in the last week, and his role should continue to grow as he ramps up.

Kon Knueppel returned from his hamstring injury, played six minutes, and then reaggravated the injury. He will be out for the next month at least, and I detailed the fallout in a video I recorded on Monday. Brandon Miller is also dealing with a groin injury, and Dennis Schroder has a hamstring strain, so the Hornets are not in the best shape right now.

Caleb Wilson suffered a hand injury on Wednesday and only played 15 minutes. It remains to be seen how long that might keep him sidelined. That would likely mean more usage for Matas Buzelis and Josh Giddey. Norman Powell is also dealing with a shoulder injury, and his name has been mentioned in trade rumors already, so the Bulls seem unlikely to play him until he's fully healed. That said, Chicago did get Nic Claxton back, and his minutes should continue to tick up over the next few weeks at the expense of Jalen Smith.

The core of the Cavs rotation remains unchanged, but Sam Merrill is out with an elbow injury, which has led to more minutes for Meleek Thomas and Tyrese Proctor. Thomas looked good against the Knicks on Wednesday, and it would not be a surprise if he continued to play himself into a larger role.

Santi Aldama returned in the last week, which led to some minutes hits for all the big men, including P.J. Washington and Daniel Gafford. That part of the rotation could get more crowded when Dereck Lively II (foot) returns as well. Cooper Flagg and Kyrie Irving continue to pace this offense, and I recorded a video about how good Flagg has been this season.

The Nuggets run a tight rotation, and not much has changed since last week. DeMar DeRozan is seeing his minutes trend down a bit as the core group gets more usage and Spencer Jones returned from his injury. DeRozan figures to be just an ancillary piece on this team, barring injury.

The Pistons are another team with a narrow and consistent rotation. Isaiah Joe and Jalen Duren both remain sidelined, but Duren has begun on-court activities, so he could return from his hamstring injury in 2-3 weeks.

Obviously the big news for the Warriors is last night's injury to Stephen Curry, which I wrote about on the website today. With Gary Payton II also out, Brandon Williams suffering a hamstring injury, and both Moses Moody and Jimmy Butler recovering from knee surgeries, the Warriors are very short-handed. We should expect to see a bump in minutes for De'Anthony Melton.

With Steven Adams and Clint Capela sidelined by injuries, the Rockets may have the narrowest rotation in the league. It seems that both players could return this weekend, so we'll see what that does to the frontcourt minutes in Houston.

With Tyrese Haliburton (hamstring) still sidelined for another two weeks, the Pacers are relying a lot on Andrew Nembhard and Aaron Nesmith. The issue is that Nesmith left Wednesday's game with knee soreness and did not return. If he were to miss time, Kelly Oubre Jr. would figure to step into a larger role.

We might be getting closer to seeing Brandon Ingram take the court for the Clippers, but that time has not yet come. That leaves the Clippers in a precarious situation. Brook Lopez's return has meant fewer minutes for Isaiah Jackson, and Derrick Jones Jr. has been playing big minutes alongside Darius Garland. There's not a lot to get excited about here.

Walker Kessler has been a tremendous addition to the Lakers, and I covered his success in a video this week. He's the clear third option behind Luka Doncic and Austin Reaves, and the rest of the team is ancillary pieces. Reaves did have X-rays on his heel, which came back negative, but he could still miss some time. Collin Sexton also had to get stitches in his forehead on Wednesday, so he may miss a game or two as that heals. If both Reaves and Sexton are out, that would lead to a bigger role for Quentin Grimes.

Things are status quo from Memphis right now. Javon Small has moved into a role in the rotation, which has impacted the minutes for Cedric Coward, and Isaiah Stewart sat out for two games earlier in the week. It's possible that Kris Murray has just been phased out of the rotation.

The Heat have a very narrow rotation, and there is little to be excited about except for consistent minutes for Bam Adebayo and Giannis Antetokounmpo. Bobby Portis Jr. has seen his minutes trend down, and Andrew Wiggins doesn't seem likely to supplant Pelle Larsson from the starting lineup. Klay Thompson is playing a lot of minutes, but he's not the same player he was a few years ago.

I mentioned last week that Kel'el Ware and Ryan Rollins were getting far fewer minutes than I expected, and that has continued. I see no reason why the Bucks will change things up now, even if they should. Brayden Burries has been stepping into a bigger role in the starting lineup and is delivering, which I discussed in a video this week.

As the Timberwolves adjust to life without both Anthony Edwards and Donte DiVincenzo, they have given more minutes to Trey Lyles and Jaylen Clark. Terrance Shannon Jr. also missed a couple of games over the weekend but returned earlier this week. The Timberwolves are going to play their starting five for as many minutes as they can every night.

The Pelicans have a deep rotation but none of it is that exciting. Dejounte Murray returned from his brief absence, but Jeremiah Fears continues to play a much larger role in this offense. Jordan Poole is now back and in the mix, but Bennedict Mathurin suffered a thumb sprain and could miss some time.

The big story in New York is that Landry Shamet has seemingly supplanted Mikal Bridges in the rotation. Bridges will start and play minutes early, but he has seen very little time on the court in the second half and played just 17 minutes in each of the last two games. Deuce McBride and Tyler Kolek are also seeing more minutes as Bridges' role shrinks for the time being until he can get back on track. Josh Hart missed two games earlier in the week but returned on Wednesday.

We know how many injuries the Thunder are dealing with, but they got some reinforcements by trading for last year's Kings first-round pick Nique Clifford. Clifford figures to play just 15-20 minutes a game for the Thunder, but that is a locked-in part of the rotation. It's also a valuable role with Ajay Mitchell now dealing with a sprained ankle. Aday Mara has stepped into a bigger role without Chet Holmgren and looked really good in his rookie season.

The Magic continue to be one of the most injury-plagued teams. After dealing with injuries to Jalen Suggs and Paolo Banchero, the Magic now have Tristan da Silva out with a calf injury. That should lead to more minutes for Jase Richardson in the short term, but the usage will fall to Franz Wagner and Anthony Black.

We knew these 76ers would battle some injuries during the season, and it's starting now. LeBron James is out on Thursday with a back injury, and Joel Embiid could miss this game with a foot injury. None of those figure to be major injuries, but with Dominick Barlow also still sidelined, it makes the 76ers just a little bit thinner than they'd like to be.

Should the Suns play Collin Gillespie more? Yes, they need a true point guard. Will they? No, it doesn't seem like they will. Ryan Dunn has seen his minutes tick up, but this is an offense without a true floor general, and the results have been what you'd expect.

With Ja Morant and Shaedon Sharpe sidelined, we've seen an uptick in minutes for Chris Youngblood. However, Youngblood missed Wednesday's game, which led to increased minutes for Damian Lillard and Jrue Holiday. The Trail Blazers would rather not have to consistently play both of those veterans over 30 minutes per game.

Keegan Murray returned from his ankle injury and is right back to his normal role in the offense. Precious Achiuwa left Tuesday's game with a leg injury, but Dylan Cardwell was taking a lot of his minutes before that happened as well. Cardwell played 29 minutes in that game and should see his minutes totals rise because of his rebounding prowess.

The Spurs are relatively healthy and very good. Devin Vassell is out with an adductor injury, and Tobias Harris remains sidelined with a calf injury, but both of them could suit up by the end of the week. The rest is status quo,

Collin Murray-Boyles missed one game earlier this week, but he has played a bigger role with Kawhi Leonard sidelined and Scott Barnes also out with a groin injury. Alijah Martin has played more as a result of Barnes' injury, but it's not expected to sideline Barnes for too long.

The Jazz just lost Lauri Markkanen to a fractured hand after already losing Jaren Jackson Jr. to a knee injury. #2831. Hear Al Michaels' emotional tribute to Dan Hicks during 'Thursday Night Football'

The sports world mourns the passing of longtime broadcaster Dan Hicks, whose death was announced on Thursday, Nov. 12. He was 64 years old.

On "Thursday Night Football," Al Michaels shared some words about his friend's legacy on the Prime Video broadcast of the game between the New York Giants and Washington Commanders. He said he and his colleagues were "shocked" to hear the news that Hicks had passed away from what NBC Sports and Hicks' wife, Hannah Storm, said in a statement was an "acute illness."

Michaels reflected on Hicks' impressive resume across multiple sports. After Hicks joined NBC Sports in 1992, Michaels said he "provided captions to so many iconic moments in golf, tennis and Notre Dame football. He and Rowdy Gaines turned the calling of swimming at multiple Olympics into an artform."

Michaels then turned to Hicks' character, remembering his kindness and joy.

"He was genial," he said. "He was compassionate, great company, had a world class sense of humor."

His voice broke as he sent his condolences to Storm and their children.

"On this very rough day for so many of us, we send our love to Hannah and your three daughters," Michaels said, choking up. "Wishing you strength and comfort."

Michaels' tribute was one of many that poured in after the news of Hicks' death broke. Leagues and fellow broadcasters posted heartfelt messages to honor their friend.

Gaines' reflection was especially saddening. The duo covered swimming for NBC for eight Olympics, beginning with the 1996 Summer Games in Atlanta. They called the 2008 Summer Games in Beijing, when the U.S. men's 4x100-meter freestyle relay swimming team, led by Michael Phelps, made one of the greatest comebacks in Olympic history to set a world record and take home the gold medal.

"My heart is broken," Gaines wrote on Instagram. "Danny wasn't just my partner in the booth for eight Olympics. He was one of my closest friends, someone I trusted with everything, both in and out of that arena. He was the glue that held us all together at all of those Games. He made me laugh like no other (person) ever has. We shared thousands of races and a friendship that went way beyond the pool deck. I'll miss his voice, his steadiness, and just having him next to me."

Hicks was scheduled to handle play-by-play duties for this weekend's Notre Dame game in South Bend, Indiana, in the battle between the Fighting Irish and Boston College.

This article originally appeared on USA TODAY: Hear Al Michaels' emotional tribute to Dan Hicks during 'Thursday Night Football'

#2830. NFL Injury Report, Week 10: Latest news, updates for Puka Nacua, George Pickens, more players for fantasy football

It's the halfway point of the 2026 NFL season and by now you likely have weathered plenty of injuries on your fantasy football rosters. We have a handful of big names we're tracking going into Week 10 while four teams are on bye — the Bears, Broncos, Buccaneers and Eagles. Below we'll update you on the latest injury news, plus provide some useful fantasy football analysis.

Sam Darnold, SEA (quad/ribs) — Out for Week 10

Drew Lock, SEA (oblique) — Should be good to start

Deshaun Watson, CLE (concussion) — Set to start

Gardner Minshew, ARI (elbow) — On IR with torn UCL

Mahomes: At the time of Mahomes' injury in Week 9, it looked as though we were watching a QB who may be done for the season. But after exiting due to an elbow injury, Mahomes was able to return in the Chiefs' 35-12 win over the Jets on Sunday. Mahomes completed 23-of-31 passes for 242 yards and two scores, posting fewer than 20 fantasy points for the fifth time in the past six games.

Kenneth Walker III continues to be the focal point of the offense as the passing game lacks depth and a vertical threat. The Chiefs face the Falcons in Week 10 and Mahomes was a full practice participant Wednesday, a good sign for him being able to play. If he's able to go, it'll be a chance for him to work with new receiver Josh Downs, who came over in a trade with the Colts on Tuesday.

Seahawks QBs: Head coach Mike Macdonald already went on the radio Monday and said Darnold will be out Week 10 against the Raiders. Lock underwent tests on an oblique injury he suffered Sunday and those came back "good," so he's in line to start again. The Raiders have been a great fantasy matchup for QBs this season, allowing the fifth-most points to the position. However, the Seattle offense has struggled a bit with lack of depth behind Jaxon Smith-Njigba. Lock is back in the QB2 streaming convo for Sunday and isn't a bad spot start if you have Caleb Williams or Bo Nix on bye.

Minshew: The Cardinals QB is on IR with a torn UCL. That means rookie Carson Beck will start. If you remember back to the preseason, Beck was actually pretty good. There's a chance this is a good move overall for the offense, particularly TE Trey McBride. But Arizona is down WR Michael Wilson (neck) and Beck won't have many other options in the passing game. In Superflex, Beck is worth a speculative add to see how he does in Week 10 against the Rams.

Willis: The Dolphins surprised us before the trade deadline by placing Willis on IR, which could mean he's done for the season this late in the year. He'll miss at least the next four games, which would mean the soonest he could be back is Week 14 against Chicago. 2025 sixth-round pick Kyle McCord is the backup and will start in Week 10 versus the Colts. Brady Cook will be the backup.

Miami's passing offense enters Week 10 ranked 30th in the NFL. Chances are it doesn't get much better with McCord under center, but we'll need to see. It's a downgrade to receivers like Malik Washington, Greg Dulcich and Caleb Douglas in the short term until we assess things. Ollie Gordon II could be leaned on a bit more, and McCord may use the RB and Dulcich as safety valves.

TreVeyon Henderson, NE (illness — ruled out)

Josh Jacobs, GB (finger) — Report: Out a max of three weeks

Travis Etienne Jr., NO (hamstring) — Chance to return this week

Bhayshul Tuten, JAX (ankle/foot) — DNP on Wednesday

Etienne: This is one to watch going into the weekend. Etienne got in a limited practice on Thursday and will likely hold a questionable tag going into Sunday's game versus the Panthers. If Etienne is active, he could be on some sort of pitch count. But he could still leech some touches away from Audric Estime, who was a trending waiver-wire add this week. Alvin Kamara also got nine touches last week against the Browns. Estime likely leads the backfield with Etienne out. But if the veteran returns, it could water down the touches between the three backs.

Patriots RBs: Stevenson exited Sunday's blowout loss to the Packers early and did not return. Henderson has missed the past two games after being hospitalized due to "unexplained neurological symptoms." Stevenson missed practice Wednesday and was limited on Thursday, but will reportedly be sidelined multiple weeks.

The Patriots face the Lions in Germany this week, which means Corey Kiner is likely to take over as the lead back. He finished with a solid 8-55-1 rushing line against Green Bay, though most of that production was on a fourth-quarter drive when the game was essentially over. Lan Larison would be the RB2 for New England. Keep an eye on this as Stevenson getting back on the practice field Thursday is telling. Maybe there's a shot he's active. But if he's out, Kiner is a decent RB3/flex play.

Jacobs: On the other side of that game, Jacobs suffered a fractured finger in the win over the Patriots and didn't return. Rushing duties were then split between Pierre Strong Jr. and Kaleb Johnson with MarShawn Lloyd out. Johnson rushed for 68 yards on 14 carries with a score. He looked pretty good running the ball for a team that has struggled to do so all season. But it was also in a blowout. Even so, Johnson is a proactive add off the waiver wire with Jacobs expected to miss time. Green Bay faces the Vikings in Week 10 before the bye and it would be hard to trust any Packers RB against one of the best defenses in the NFL. Johnson will be a risky flex play against Minnesota in Week 10. Strong isn't a bad add in deeper PPR formats and keep an eye on Lloyd, who could be back soon.

Puka Nacua, LAR (knee/turf toe) — Placed on IR

Michael Wilson, ARI (neck) — Out for season

Luther Burden III, CHI (concussion) — on bye

Nacua: The wideout was placed on injured reserve Thursday, meaning he will miss at least the next four games with knee sprains and turf toe. The Rams are already without Davante Adams, but McVay did offer hope that the veteran WR could return from a ruptured hamstring as soon as Week 13. For now, there isn't much WR depth. Konata Mumpfield, Tutu Atwell and the trio of tight ends — Tyler Higbee, Colby Parkinson and Davis Allen — will need to step up. We'd throw Terrance Ferguson into that group, but he remains on IR and isn't expected back in Week 10. L.A. should lean more on its run game with Kyren Williams and Blake Corum.

With Nacua out, it's a downgrade to the passing game and Matthew Stafford's fantasy outlook. It's a slight bump up for Williams and Corum, both of whom see plenty of work in the passing game plenty. The Rams take on the Cardinals before their bye in Week 11, which is an easy draw for the run game. Mumpfield isn't a bad waiver-wire add, along with Atwell in deeper formats. But don't expect any receiver to get the type of volume Nacua did.

Michael Wilson: The WR will miss the rest of the season due to a neck injury. It's a tough blow for managers who were hoping for more following the Marvin Harrison Jr. trade. Instead, Wilson struggled, failing to reach double-digit fantasy points in four of the past five games. With Jalen Brooks and Kendrick Bourne on injured reserve, Arizona is very, very thin at WR.

Simi Fehoko, Reggie Virgil and Devin Duvernay all played significant snaps in Week 9 against Seattle. None of them did much, while Trey McBride was obviously the main target for Gardner Minshew. Though, Minshew is on IR with a torn UCL and the team will start rookie Carson Beck at QB this week.

Pickens: The Cowboys held Pickens out of their win over the Colts in Week 9. As a result, CeeDee Lamb went off for 10-195-1 while third-string WR Ryan Flournoy was quiet with two catches for 53 yards. Dallas leaned on the run game, leading most of the first half. It's clear the offense missed Pickens and will need him ready for a key NFC matchup with the Niners in Week 10. Pickens was a limited practice participant Wednesday. If Pickens is out again, Flournoy remains a flex option, while Lamb would remain a target hog and elite WR1.

#2829. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2828. Widespread Microsoft outage takes down Outlook and other 365 services

Microsoft suffered a widespread outage on Thursday that impacted thousands of users across regions, according to outage-tracking website Downdetector.com.

The outage began shortly before 1 p.m. and impacted Microsoft 365 services, Outlook and Teams. At the time, about 130,000 users reported issues with Outlook.

Amazon Web Services and Google users also reported outages on the site.

"We are aware and investigating reports of users experiencing issues with Microsoft 365 services, primarily issues with Exchange Online," a Microsoft spokesperson wrote in a statement to CNBC. "We appreciate our customers' patience while we work to restore services as soon as possible."

Microsoft shared updates on the outage from its Microsoft 365 Status account on social media platform X.

Amazon wrote on X that services were "operating normally" and an "unrelated disruption elsewhere on the internet is prompting some inaccurate news reports." The company referred users to its health dashboard for "accurate data."

At 4 p.m., Microsoft said it had "validated an alternative service health check method" that brought some affected servers back online.

"We're now applying this change more broadly," Microsoft wrote. "We're also preparing automated recovery systems to continually detect and restart unresponsive servers, to maintain service stability while this change progresses."

Microsoft initially said infrastructure "elevated load across multiple regions" was responsible for the connectivity problems.

The company later determined that the elevated load was caused by a "number of servers being unexpectedly unavailable; straining the remaining infrastructure" and that some servers were failing automated health checks.

"We've taken steps to prevent additional servers from being impacted while we work to return the affected servers to service," Microsoft wrote.

#2827. Orioles Predicted to Land Three-Time All-Star in Major Offseason Move

The Baltimore Orioles were one of the more frustrating teams in the baseball landscape this past season.

After a strong offseason, the Orioles couldn't do much with it, falling short of reaching the playoffs this year. The big move for Baltimore was signing slugger Pete Alonso, giving this team a legitimate offensive presence.

However, it wasn't enough for this group, and now the front office will be tasked with trying to get this team back to the playoffs. Many expect the Orioles to be active again this offseason, addressing some of the problems from the 2026 season.

One name that could be of interest for the Orioles is veteran outfielder Randy Arozarena, who will be a free agent. Arozarena isn't expected to return to the Seattle Mariners this offseason, opening the door for a team like Baltimore to strike.

MLB analyst Ryan Finkelstein of Just Baseball linked the Orioles with Arozarena, predicting the slugger will land in Baltimore. Arozarena is one of the better free agents on the market, and the Orioles could look to make another splash this offseason.

"From 2021 through 2025, Randy Arozarena averaged 22 home runs a season with a 122 wRC+. He was very consistent at being around 20-to-25% better than a league-average hitter by wRC+, while also adding value on the basepaths. Arozarena has reached the 20-20 club in each of his six full seasons in MLB.

That consistency alone would have gotten Arozarena paid, but to make his free agent case even better, Randy went out and posted the best season of his career in his walk year. By wRC+, Arozarena was the third-best qualified hitter in baseball, trailing only the two MVPs, Yordan Alvarez and Pete Crow-Armstrong. With that platform year, Arozarena is a safe bet to be the highest-paid free agent bat on the market. Last offseason, the Baltimore Orioles ponied up and signed Pete Alonso on a five-year deal, and he immediately became their best player in 2026," Finkelstein wrote.

Arozarena was one of the few bright spots for the Mariners this past season, giving life to this offense during a down year. For the year, the slugger hit .281 with 26 home runs, 79 RBIs, and 26 stolen bases, while posting an OPS of .865.

Given where the Orioles are right now, the team does need to add more offense around Alonso, and Arozarena could bring the energy to this team. This was something that Baltimore was lacking last season, and Arozarena is one of the more fun players in the entire league.

While the Orioles could go after pitching this offseason, addressing the inconsistent offense does need to be done. Arozarena could be one of the bigger pieces added to this team, and he could be lethal in a lineup alongside Alonso and the others that Baltimore has.

Baltimore will have to open up the checkbook again to get a deal like this done, but it could be very worth it for this team. Overall, Arozarena feels like he could fit in well with the Orioles, helping them get back to the playoffs as early as next season.

#2826. Rams place Puka Nacua on IR after all. Where does LA go from here?

Puka Nacua is head to injured reserve after all.

The Los Angeles Rams officially placed their star wide receiver on IR on Thursday, Nov. 12, a day after head coach Sean McVay told reporters that Nacua would miss significant time but avoid IR due to "roster semantics." Per NFL rules, a team can designate up to eight players for return from injured reserve in a single season.

The Rams, plagued by injuries all year, have already designated three players – Myles Garrett, rookie tackle Keagen Trost and running back Ronnie Rivers – and a fourth, Terrance Ferguson, is expected back in a couple weeks after tweaking his hamstring in practice last Friday. Kobie Turner and Davante Adams figure to be the fifth and sixth players to return for IR, with Nacua presumably being the seventh in at least four weeks from now.

Nacua injured his PCL and MCL during a jet sweep on the final offensive play in their Week 9 win over the Washington Commanders, in which he put up 150 yards and a touchdown on 14 receptions. WR Brennan Presley was signed from the practice squad and the Rams signed Tyler Scott to the practice squad in corresponding moves. Xavier Smith was signed from the San Francisco 49ers' practice squad on Tuesday after Jordan Whittington was placed on IR with a back injury.

Assuming Nacua returns in the earliest window possible, Week 14 against the 49ers, that means the Rams would only be able to bring back one of Whittington, offensive lineman Justin Dedich, running back Dean Connors and lineman Steve Avila (who has not been officially placed on IR yet) barring any other major injuries.

McVay told reporters on Wednesday that the team hopes Adams can return after Week 12 vs. the Green Bay Packers, a welcome bit of good news for a receiver room that has been decimated in the last few weeks.

"Guys will be asked to step up," McVay told reporters on Monday ahead of the trade deadline. "Puka has always had a pretty large target share based on his production and the different things that he does and because of the way that you're able to get him the football because of some of the unselfish stuff he does in the run game.

"... There are other guys that are very capable of doing some good stuff for us. We'll look to see those guys step up and it'll be great opportunity for them in spite of it being a bummer for those guys that are out."

#2824. Cunningham's incredible night — 52 points, 11 assists, no turnovers — saves Pistons in double OT

WASHINGTON (AP) — The Washington Wizards kept making last-second shots, forcing Cade Cunningham to play an extra 10 minutes.

All that did was turn a great night into a historic one for the Detroit star.

Cunningham scored 52 points and had 11 assists, leading the Pistons to a 144-140 win in double overtime Thursday night. He became the first player since at least 1978 with at least 50 points and 10 assists in a game with no turnovers. In a game that was tight pretty much throughout, Cunningham kept mistakes to a minimum while still being incredibly productive.

"It was the best I've ever done it in my NBA career for sure," Cunningham said.

Cunningham had only 22 points after three quarters before scoring 18 in the fourth. That still wasn't enough because Trae Young's 3-pointer at the buzzer tied the game at 121. Then Washington scored the first seven points of overtime, but there was still plenty of time for Cunningham. A jumper, two free throws and a 3-pointer later, he'd tied the game at 130.

Duncan Robinson put the Pistons ahead with a jumper, but AJ Dybantsa — the top overall pick in this year's draft — tied it with less than a second left for Washington.

There were four players in this game who were picked No. 1 overall — Dybantsa, Anthony Davis, Deandre Ayton and Cunningham. Davis had 30 points and 15 rebounds for the Wizards before fouling out in the second overtime after Cunningham made a 3 to put the Pistons up 135-132.

Young scored 37 points and Alex Sarr added 30. The Wizards had not had three 30-point scorers in a game since Nov. 18, 1969, when the team was called the Baltimore Bullets and Mike Davis, Earl Monroe and Gus Johnson formed the high-scoring trio against the San Diego Rockets.

Cunningham equaled the fourth-highest scoring total in franchise history for the Pistons. Jerry Stackhouse scored 57 points in a 2001 game, Kelly Tripucka had 56 in 1983, Dave Bing had 54 in 1971 and George Yardley scored 52 in 1958.

No other Detroit player scored more points than Daniss Jenkins' 21, and he didn't have any after the third quarter. Down the stretch, Cunningham took over, even overshadowing teammate Ausar Thompson's first career triple-double.

"He led us through the fourth quarter and overtimes," said Thompson, who had 17 points, 11 assists and 11 rebounds. "He did what he does. One person can't just guard him."

#2823. As Nvidia reels from Huang's affairs, who stands to gain?

Nvidia's leadership crisis is creating an opening for its biggest rivals as well as partners, as investors bet that Jensen Huang's departure could loosen the AI chip giant's grip on an industry it has long dominated.

From AMD to Broadcom and even partner TSMC, chipmakers are emerging as potential beneficiaries of the upheaval, with market watchers pointing to a possible redistribution of manufacturing capacity and a narrowing of Nvidia's technological lead.

"What you're seeing in markets now is a bit of a rotation into other advanced chip design companies, on the assumption that this will enable other companies effectively to catch up," said Mark Franklin, head of multi-asset solutions for Asia at Manulife Investment Management.

That shift comes as Nvidia grapples with the fallout from Huang's resignation, following revelations about his multiple affairs including with a woman believed by the U.S. intelligence community to be a Chinese spy.

Huang's departure could give competitors an opportunity to close the gap as Nvidia navigates leadership uncertainty, Franklin highlighted, adding that the rotation was already helping fuel positive momentum in other semiconductor stocks.

Farzin Azam, managing director of equities trading at Mizuho Americas, said the shift was evident on trading desks, with investors moving toward chipmakers less exposed to Nvidia's management turmoil.

"Names such as TSMC, names such as Broadcom, names such as Advanced Micro will come as winners of this because they don't have this overhang of a management disruption," Azam told CNBC.

Nvidia shares have plunged 11% so far this week, while rivals AMD and Broadcom have gained 30% and 24%, respectively.

TSMC, which manufactures Nvidia's most advanced processors, has also rallied 11% over the same period, as it stands to benefit, irrespective of which chip designer gains or loses. A rotation away from AI names perceived as risky also positions TSMC as a relatively safer play for those bullish on the overall theme.

The rotation away from Nvidia has also extended into less speculative corners of the semiconductor industry, including analog chipmakers such as Texas Instruments, Analog Devices and Infineon, as investors adopt a more defensive stance, he said.

Texas Instruments has climbed about 13% this week, while Analog Devices and Germany's Infineon have advanced 12% and 14%, respectively.

Beyond the immediate market reaction, Huang's exit could fundamentally reshape competition across the AI supply chain, according to Dylan Patel, chief executive at semiconductor research firm SemiAnalysis.

Patel said Huang had personally cultivated relationships with manufacturers and component suppliers, helping Nvidia secure scarce production capacity ahead of rivals.

That advantage could now come under pressure as companies including Google and Amazon strive to expand AI chip production.

The two hyperscalers were already gaining ground in securing supply-chain capacity relative to Nvidia, Patel said, and could accelerate those efforts without Huang's influence over suppliers.

"If Nvidia can't muster the supply chain, then technology is the only competitive advantage. And without Jensen, can they maintain technology advantages? Given they're only a year ahead of their biggest competitors, it's not like the gap is massive," Patel said.

The challenge is particularly acute as hyperscalers increasingly develop proprietary AI chips to reduce their reliance on Nvidia, while AMD and other semiconductor companies race to capture a larger slice of the market.

Yet the upheaval doesn't necessarily signal an end to the broader AI investment boom.

Franklin noted that major technology companies are projecting higher AI spending in 2027, with further increases possible in 2028, signaling the investment cycle remains structurally intact even as investors reassess which companies stand to benefit.

The bigger question is whether growing competition and the proliferation of open-source technologies eventually commoditize AI capabilities, putting pressure on chip prices and investment returns, he said.

For Nvidia, the immediate outlook may depend less on its earnings than on whether its next leadership team can reassure investors.

Azam said the stock appeared inexpensive relative to its earnings growth prospects, but warned that ongoing investigations and management uncertainty could limit any sustained recovery.

He expects Nvidia's earnings due Nov. 17 to remain strong, with investors likely to focus more closely on management's plans for navigating the crisis.

The result could be a reshuffling of winners within the semiconductor industry, with Franklin saying that investors were becoming more comfortable staying invested while rotating and diversifying their portfolios, instead of abandoning the market altogether.

#2820. Alice Huang tied to Nvidia’s biggest Chinese competitor

Alleged Chinese spy Alice Huang may have owned stakes in businesses in China that benefited Nvidia's biggest competitor in China, Huawei, at the same time that she was in business with Nvidia and also reportedly having a romantic affair with its ousted CEO Jensen Huang.

The connection between Alice Huang and Huawei was first discovered by analysts at private intelligence firm Tesari. The firm exclusively shared its findings with CNBC. Tesari, which uses artificial intelligence to collect evidence and specializes in national security investigations around the world, provides intelligence research to private companies and the U.S. government.

This previously unreported entanglement may raise questions about Alice Huang's intent and conduct in her relationship with the CEO and whether Jensen Huang knew his romantic partner was closely connected with his competition. Jensen Huang has acknowledged having an affair, though the precise timing of his relationship with Alice Huang is unknown.

The mysterious saga of Alice Huang involves a tangle of interlocking corporations and transactions that show her connection to entities that ultimately provided computing power to Huawei in China.

Huawei is known as a Chinese government "state champion" in the technology and telecommunications sectors, meaning it receives enormous financial support from the government in Beijing so it can lower prices and undercut global competitors. It is considered a key part of the Chinese government's push for global technical dominance and is closely linked to the Chinese Communist Party.

Businesses connected to Alice Huang were linked with supplying Nvidia computing power to Huawei, according to documents reviewed by CNBC, around the same time she and Jensen Huang were first publicly seen together, in December 2023.

In 2024, Nvidia said Huawei was a top competitor, specifically in artificial intelligence chips including graphics processing units, central processing units and networking chips.

"This activity, which surfaced through our analysis of Alice's network, warrants a national security and [technology] counterproliferation investigation," said Brian Andersen, Tesari's vice president for national security applications and a former supervisory special agent with Homeland Security Investigations.

If the Commerce Department's Bureau of Industry Security and Homeland Security Investigations found evidence that anyone transferred "controlled equipment" to Huawei without authorization, potential charges might include export-control violations, conspiracy and smuggling, Andersen said.

The connection between Alice Huang and Huawei is so convoluted that analysts at Tesari told CNBC that based on their previous investigative experience, they suspect it was designed to deliberately obscure the relationship.

Nvidia and Huawei did not respond to CNBC's requests for comment. Jensen Huang did not respond to a voicemail left on a mobile phone. Alice Huang could not be reached.

According to Chinese corporate records and media reports compiled by Tesari and provided to CNBC, Alice Huang, whose Chinese name is Huang Le and who according to documents at the time was based in Hong Kong, in 2019 sold a cloud business she owned called Osmanthus to a Chinese gaming company called 7Road. A subsidiary of 7Road, which was based in Singapore, was spun off as "Megaspeed" in 2023. Megaspeed would go on to become a key Nvidia customer, helping facilitate at least $7.5 billion in Nvidia chip shipments to Chinese firms, The Information reported Tuesday.

As early as 2016, Alice Huang had also become a co-owner of a financial firm known as Shanghai Hexi, which heavily invests in tech and data centers. In 2021, Hexi in turn acquired 27 percent of a company called Shanghai Century Longteng, a data center developer, which Hexi held until 2024. That firm owned a controlling 50.1 percent stake in a data center facility called Shenzhen Yifeng Technology Co. Ltd. in mainland China.

The key customer of that data center was Huawei Cloud, the Shenzhen, China-based subsidiary of Huawei Technologies.

In addition, Alice Huang's firm Hexi guaranteed the loans required to finance the Shenzhen data center, which did business with Huawei.

Hexi did not immediately respond to a message via its LinkedIn page sent outside of normal business hours.

CNBC translated corporate records and Chinese news articles in Mandarin that Tesari provided.

It's not clear if the Shenzhen data center was able to acquire Nvidia GPUs, but its majority owner began purchasing the chips in 2023. That company is known as Zhejiang Century Huatong Group and is a business partner of Alice Huang's Hexi through their joint ownership of Century Longteng.

That made Alice Huang a part owner of a data center providing computing power to Huawei. And that was happening at around the same time the first known sightings of Alice Huang and Jensen Huang in public together.

And that raises the question of why Huawei, which has access to Chinese-made chips, would need to rent computing power from an outside entity.

Hexi exited its stake in the data center development company in 2024, but individuals within Alice Huang's network are still connected to the Shenzhen data center. Her husband Kang Wei is a longtime business associate who founded Hexi, where Alice Huang previously held an executive role. In 2022, Kang became an executive of Century Longteng, which owns the controlling stake in the Shenzhen data center, and he also attended the company's signing of the Huawei agreement.

#2816. Failed Daiyan Henley trade exposes a flaw in the NFL's trade deadline

The trade deadline is the trade deadline. Except when it’s not the trade deadline.

The strange situation involving the failed trade of Chargers linebacker Daiyan Henley to the Broncos reveals a major flaw in the NFL’s deadline-day machinery.

Adam Schefter of ESPN has provided the details (which we have confirmed to be accurate) in the most nonchalant, innocuous terms possible: “It is not uncommon for one team to have some medical concerns about a player while another does not. In this case, Denver had concerns about Henley following his physical. Even then, the Broncos still wanted Henley with Denver and LA discussing revised trade alternatives throughout the day, taking into account those concerns. In the end, no agreement was reached. The deal was off.”

Here’s what it means. The Chargers and Broncos struck a deal for Henley and independently communicated the terms to the NFL before the trade deadline of 4:00 p.m. ET on Tuesday, November 10. Like all trades, the Henley deal was subject to the player passing a physical.

The Broncos discovered an issue with Henley’s knee. It has, we’re told, arthritis. (As one source with neither the Chargers nor the Broncos told PFT, most if not all starting linebackers in the NFL have some degree of arthritis in their knees.)

The NFL allows trade terms to be revised after the deadline, to an extent. Per a league spokesperson: “We would permit them to change the pick compensation. They would not be able to add a player or trade a different player.”

Instead of concluding that the physical had been failed and canceling the deal (like the Ravens did with the trade for Raiders defensive end Maxx Crosby in March), the Broncos were willing to proceed with revised terms. In other words, for something less than a fourth-round pick in 2027 and a fifth-round pick in 2028, they’d still do the deal.

That’s not how it should work after the deadline. The window has closed. The deals are done, or they’re not. After 4:00 p.m. ET on November 10, the only question left to answer is whether the player passes or fails the physical.

As one source explained it, deadline-day deals have gone through in the past with terms that were revised after the deadline.

Based on Schefter’s reporting, that’s what happened here. The Broncos had “medical concerns,” but not enough to reject the player. The Broncos and Chargers discussed “revised trade alternatives” on Wednesday — the day after the window for doing trades ended.

The Chargers could have taken less in order to get the deal done. They chose not to do so. The fact that the rules allow trade terms to be renegotiated after the deadline opened the door for the Broncos to squeeze the Chargers.

The situation creates an opportunity for shenanigans. If, for example, Team A is preparing to trade a player to Team B at the deadline for a fourth-round pick and a sixth-round pick, Team C can swoop in and offer a fourth-round pick and a fifth-round pick. Then, when the physical is performed after the deadline, Team C can say, “Well, we have medical concerns. If we don’t agree to reduce the package to a fourth-round pick and a sixth-round pick, we’re going to fail the player on his physical.”

That’s not to say the Broncos did it deliberately. Obviously, however, they remained interested in Henley (who has started all eight games this year and has participated in 89 percent of the snaps) at something less than the agreed terms.

The point is that this little-known ability to extend the trading period by giving the acquiring team the ability to use the physical to leverage better terms shouldn’t exist. When a deal is done at the deadline, the trade should either go through for the agreed terms or it shouldn’t.

It opens the door to gamesmanship. The team that has traded the player has no alternatives. It can’t trade the player to someone else. It either has to accept reduced terms or try to put the toothpaste back in the tube.

The Vikings, for example, could have tried to do that to the Raiders regarding quarterback Kirk Cousins. “Well, we found something in Kirk’s leg that we don’t like. We’re not comfortable giving up a third-round pick. How about a fifth-round pick instead?”

What would the Raiders have done at that point? They’d already turned the page to Fernando Mendoza, and they spent Tuesday and Wednesday getting him ready to play.

The rule needs to change. The ability to revisit trade terms after the deadline has passed should be eliminated. Either the deal gets done as negotiated, or it doesn’t.

Hovering over all of this is the subjectivity that can result in a team failing a player on a physical if it wants that outcome. It’s an easy way out for a team that has buyer’s remorse. (The Bengals, for example, could have determined that Eagles defensive end Arnold Ebiketie failed his physical, if the Bengals hadn’t realized until after the trade terms were finalized that the Bengals would lose a 2027 third-round compensatory pick if the trade became official.)

At some point, it makes sense for the physicals not to be conducted by team doctors but by an independent, unaffiliated physician. But that’s for another day. For now, the immediate problem that needs to be solved is the ability of teams to renegotiate trade terms after the trade deadline.

No one benefits from that, and unscrupulous teams could use it to their advantage.

#2814. Christian Gonzalez: I'm going into Sunday with the same confidence I always have

Patriots cornerback Christian Gonzalez is coming off a terrible game against the Packers, but he said on Thursday that it won’t be hanging over his head in Munich this Sunday.

Gonzalez was no match for Packers wideouts Matthew Golden and Christian Watson as they posted 10 catches for 187 yards with Gonzalez in coverage during Green Bay’s 45-27 win in New England. It was the first time that Next Gen Stats has ever recorded a cornerback allowing more than 80 receiving yards to two receivers in the same game and head coach Mike Vrabel said the team and the corner expect better play than they got on Sunday.

On Thursday, Gonzalez was asked about his level of confidence that he can provide that kind of play.

“As confident as I always am,” Gonzalez said, via 98.5 The Sports Hub. “It’s a week-to-week league. Like I’ve told you all week, it’s a new week. The same confidence I always have is the same confidence I’m always going with.”

Gonzalez has had more good days than bad days over the course of his NFL career, but the film of last week is the freshest and the Lions could use Amon-Ra St. Brown and Jameson Williams to put Gonzalez to the test early in this weekend’s game.

#2813. NFL news, live updates: Rams officially place Puka Nacua on injured reserve with knee injury

The 2026 NFL trade deadline is officially in the rearview mirror, and we're on to Week 10, where we'll see a couple of players in new uniforms this week — and some in their old uniforms.

Kirk Cousins rejoined the Minnesota Vikings this week via a trade with the Las Vegas Raiders. He will presumably don purple this weekend against the Green Bay Packers — the very team he last faced with Minnesota, when he suffered a season-ending Achilles tear. He'll look to give the offense a much-needed boost to complement Brian Flores' defense after the team's 6-2 start.

With the trade of Cousins, the Raiders officially named 2026 No. 1 pick Fernando Mendoza as their starter moving forward. He'll face a tough schedule to kick off his career as a starting quarterback, with the Seattle Seahawks and Denver Broncos on deck in Weeks 10 and 11.

Follow along with Yahoo Sports for the latest NFL news, injury updates and more ahead of Week 10.

After a great deal of speculation was made that Jonathan Taylor could be moved ahead of the NFL trade deadline, the Indianapolis Colts are reportedly standing pat.

The team will not trade him ahead of the deadline, NFL insider Tom Pelissero reported Tuesday afternoon.

Pelissero reported that the Colts had received multiple offers for Taylor of a second-round pick-plus, but instead, the team will move forward with him as a foundational piece within the locker room as they look to rebuild.

Any concerns that Justin Herbert might be limited by his injured left middle finger were likely put to rest with the quarterback not listed on the Los Angeles Chargers injury report for Thursday.

After getting injured and leaving during the fourth quarter of the Chargers' 44-22 loss to the Houston Texans, imaging exams showed no fracture in Herbert's finger.

However, the Chargers' injury report was extensive with 18 players listed, including receiver Ladd McConkey (back), edge rusher Khalil Mack (rest) and linebacker Daiyan Henley (personal), who did not practice. Tight end David Njoku (fibula) was a limited participant.

The Chargers (0-8) will try to get their first win of the season on Monday night versus the Baltimore Ravens (4-5).

The Los Angeles Rams have reportedly placed All-Pro wide receiver Puka Nacua on injured reserve with a knee injury suffered last week, per ESPN's Adam Schefter.

That will have him out for the next four games, after Sean McVay previously noted Nacua would miss a "significant" period of time with the injury. It took some time to play out, however, as the team dealt with what McVay called "roster semantics."

Kirk Cousins' return to Minnesota will be marked with a familiar number: The team announced Thursday that their new quarterback will wear No. 8 going forward. That's the number Cousin wore with the Vikings when he played for them from 2018-2023.

Minnesota safety Jakobe Thomas, who was wearing No. 8 earlier this season, will switch to No. 6 to accommodate Cousins return.

Riley Patterson will kick for the Ravens this week in their Monday Night Football matchup with the Chargers, according to the NFL Network's Mike Garafolo. Patterson spent the past two seasons with the Dolphins, but was released last month.

The Patriots will be playing the Lions in Munich, Germany, on Sunday.

Running back TreVeyon Henderson did not practice on Wednesday, and with an upcoming flight across the pond, the team has already ruled him out.

Henderson was listed on the injury report as dealing with an illness. The running back revealed last Friday that he recently experienced "an unexpected and unusual medical emergency involving a series of unexplained neurological symptoms." Henderson was hospitalized, but has said that test results have been positive and reassuring.

Starting running back Rhamondre Stevenson also didn't practice on Wednesday due to a knee injury, but has not yet been ruled out, though he is expected to miss a few weeks.

Sean McVay delivered a mix bag of news Wednesday regarding injuries to the team's top two receivers.

Davante Adams could be back sooner than expected from a hamstring injury, potentially in Week 13 vs. the Chiefs. Puka Nacua, meanwhile, will remain sidelined for a "significant period of time" with knee and toe injuries.

Etienne has been out since Week 3 due to a hamstring injury and has since been on injured reserve.

His return to practice opens his 21-day window to return to action, meaning the Saints have three weeks to activate him to the 53-man roster or shut him down for the season.

Skattebo missed Sunday's win over the Eagles coming out of the team's bye week. The running back practiced this week, which sets him up for a return Thursday night against the Commanders.

McCarthy was sacked five times in the game and suffered a rib injury that briefly took him out of action. He would return and finished 12-for-19 for 127 yards passing.

"I feel really good," McCarthy said this week. "Obviously, Sunday, it's football, it's a physical game, but just credit to the athletic training staff, getting me back to feeling really good as quick as possible."

The 49ers acquired Keenan Allen ahead of Tuesday's trade deadline because of the various injuries the team has at the position.

San Francisco head coach Kyle Shanahan said on Wednesday that Mike Evans remains week-to-week due to an Achilles injury. At this point, he has not been added to IR, so Evans could suit up this week against the Cowboys, but he is starting the week by not practicing.

Shanahan added that currently there is no timeline for Evans' return.

The Rams are expected to be without star wide receiver Puka Nacua for more than a month after suffering two knee sprains in the team's most recent game.

Nacua, who would normally be added to IR to free up a roster spot, won't be, according to head coach Sean McVay. L.A. is still expecting Nacua to miss extended time, but NFL teams are only allowed eight player activations from IR per season.

If Nacua gets added, with the intention of activating him in December, he would take up an activation from another player that the Rams were planning on activating, which would rule them out for the year.

L.A. currently has WR Davante Adams, G Justin Dedich, DT Poona Ford and DE Kobie Turner on IR and the Rams likely want to be able to activate all of them later this year. Los Angeles has already used four activations with EDGE Myles Garrett, TE Terrance Ferguson, OT Keagan Trost and RB Ronnie Rivers.

The 49ers acquired Keenan Allen ahead of Tuesday's trade deadline to help bolster the banged-up wide receiver room.

The only problem is that Allen could still miss time this season. Kyle Shanahan acknowledged to reporters on Wednesday that the veteran wideout could still face league discipline this season stemming from his DUI in August.

According to the current CBA, a violation of the league's substance-abuse policy, which would include a DUI, comes with a minimum three-game suspension. Additionally, Allen's BAC was so high that the league can increase the suspension if it so chooses.

The league has reportedly been waiting for the DUI case against Allen to play out legally before issuing the suspension.

At 3-6, and with a rash of injuries — including one to their most recent QB1 — the Arizona Cardinals are turning to rookie Carson Beck.

Arizona benched and then traded Jacoby Brissett. His replacement, Gardner Minshew, suffered a UCL injury this past week in Seattle during a loss to the Seahawks.

In their absence, the No. 65 overall pick in this year's draft is getting the nod in the desert.

Beck will make his first career NFL start on Sunday when the Los Angeles Rams come to town, head coach Mike LaFleur said Wednesday. The former Georgia and Miami star will be tasked with moving the ball against a defense that features premier edge rusher Myles Garrett and stout defensive tackle Aaron Donald. Beck will have to do so with a thin receiving corps.

Pittsburgh head coach Mike McCarthy announced on Wednesday that the team is opening the 21-day practice window for defensive tackle Derrick Harmon.

The 23-year-old defender has been sidelined because of a sprained foot he suffered against the Bengals in Week 4. In limited action this season, he has 12 tackles, including 1.5 sacks and a forced fumble.

Pittsburgh is allowing the seventh-fewest yards per game and the third-fewest rush yards per game this season.

The Patriots will cross the pond this weekend to take on the Detroit Lions in Munich, Germany, on Sunday morning.

New England wide receiver A.J. Brown will travel with the team, head coach Mike Vrabel said. While this doesn't automatically mean he will play in Sunday's game, Vrabel noted it gives him the chance.

Brown has missed the Patriots' last two games due to a knee injury. The wide receiver has only played in three games for New England this season, as he missed the early part of the season because of a sprained ankle in Week 1.

The wide receiver has caught nine of his 20 targets for 101 yards. Brown has yet to score a touchdown this year.

Tuesday's trade deadline was highlighted by the return of quarterback Kirk Cousins to the Minnesota Vikings.

Despite the move, on Wednesday, Minnesota head coach Kevin O'Connell wasn't ready to commit to Cousins as the starter this weekend against the Packers.

This could just be gamesmanship by O'Connell, but he told the media about Cousins starting: "I will say [we're] excited about the opportunity of spending every moment we can between now and then to get him comfortable and allow him to play for us this Sunday. That's what we're working towards, but also want to be fair to Kirk and make sure he feels good about it going to Lambeau Field."

Green Bay head coach Matt LaFleur said in his Wednesday press conference that he was surprised that the Raiders didn't get more for Cousins. "I was surprised [the Raiders] didn't get more for him, quite honestly. I was shocked when I saw what they traded him for."

Green Bay Packers running back Josh Jacobs may not end up on injured reserve despite needing surgery on his broken ring finger.

Jacobs, who is set to have that procedure later this week, could be back as soon as 2-3 weeks, according to NFL Network's Ian Rapoport, who also reported that Green Bay will decide Saturday if IR is necessary.

The Packers play the Minnesota Vikings on Sunday. But then they have a bye. There's a chance Jacobs returns for the following week's road game against the Los Angeles Rams.

The Chiefs will stick with Harrison Butker in Week 10, giving the kicker another chance to turn things around after weeks of struggles, head coach Andy Reid announced Wednesday.

After Sunday's win over the Jets, in which Butker missed another field goal attempt (a 51-yarder), Reid said that he would "look at everything" to assess whether it was time to move on. Evidently, Reid decided to stick with his long-time kicker.

Butker, who has spent his entire 10-year NFL career with the Chiefs, has seen his field goal accuracy crater this season. He's missed seven field goals in eight games so far this year, going 12-of-19 on attempts. That 63% completion rate is a noticeable dip from his previous decade of experience.

New York Jets wide receiver Garrett Wilson will be back at practice Wednesday, albeit in a limited capacity while coming off a concussion, per ESPN's Rich Cimini.

Wilson was sidelined during last week's 35-12 road loss to the Kansas City Chiefs.

He absorbed a blow to the head from safety Jeremy Chinn late in a Week 8 defeat to the Las Vegas Raiders. Wilson then reported concussion symptoms the following Monday, Jets head coach Aaron Glenn told reporters.

Wilson has piled up 43 receptions, 527 receiving yards and 3 receiving touchdowns this season.

The Cleveland Browns are expected to turn back to quarterback Deshaun Watson in Week 10, according to The Athletic.

The move was expected, as reports emerged Monday saying Watson would start for the team after clearing concussion protocol.

Browns head coach Todd Monken shot down those rumors Monday, however, saying he had yet to make a decision on the team's starter in Week 10.

With Watson sidelined, Shedeur Sanders drew his first start of the 2026 season. He nearly led the Browns to an overtime win in that start.

Watson, who missed the contest due to a concussion, reportedly cleared league protocol Friday. Despite that, the was not active for Sunday's game against the New Orleans Saints.

#2812. Warriors G-League affiliate re-signs Dalen Terry after Thunder stint

The Santa Cruz Warriors have reacquired wing Dalen Terry after he was released by the Oklahoma City Thunder, per Boardwalk Buckets. Terry will be available for the team's home opener tomorrow. Terry signed a two-year non-guaranteed deal with the Thunder less than two weeks ago, but was waived after appearing in just two games following the Niq Clifford trade. Terry was slated to be a centerpiece of the Santa Cruz (the Golden State Warriors G-League affiliate) team prior to the Thunder move. He now will reprise that role after getting a brief pay raise from Oklahoma City.

Terry appeared in two games with the Thunder, recording 2 points, 6 rebounds, 1 assist, 1 steal, and 2 turnovers in 11 minutes. Terry's deal with OKC was fully non-guaranteed so he will only receive a small pro-rated portion of the league minimum.

The Chicago Bulls drafted Terry with the 18th overall pick back in the 2022 NBA Draft out of Arizona. He appeared in 218 games over his first four seasons, but was unable to show enough potential to convince a team to dedicate a rotation spot for him heading into this season. The Bulls had declined Terry's fifth-year option, and dealt him to the 76ers at last year's deadline. After failing to get an NBA deal in free agency, he joined the Warriors a series of deals over the summer.

A 6'6 wing who just turned 24, Terry remains an intriguing 3-and-D prospect. The fact that an organization as renowned for scouting and development as the Thunder was willing to give him a trial window further endorses that side of things. Nevertheless, he has averaged just 3.5 points, 1.7 rebounds, and 1.2 assists on 44.3%/31.4%/63.8% shooting in 11.2 minutes per game over his career.

#2811. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

Catellier ranks No. 667 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 9.60%. See Williams Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2810. Kirk Cousins will wear No. 8 again for Vikings

Vikings fans won’t need to buy new Kirk Cousins jerseys.

The team has announced that Cousins will wear No. 8 in his second stint in Minnesota. Safety Jakobe Thomas, who most recently wore No. 8, will switch to No. 6.

Thomas, a rookie who played last year at the University of Miami, wore No. 6 during his time at Middle Tennessee State.

When asked about the number situation on Wednesday, Cousins told reporters: “I’m confident that Jakobe and I can have a healthy and mature conversation and figure this out.” (Whether that conversation included a healthy and mature transfer of cash to Thomas remains to be seen.)

Regardless, Cousins needed to be back in No. 8. Thomas, who has yet to play in a regular-season game, surely knew that.

Although it could have been a moneymaker for the Vikings and for Cousins if he’d landed in a new number. Vikings fans are sufficiently giddy to consider buying another one.

#2809. Jacoby Brissett: The last week has definitely been a crash course

Jacoby Brissett has been a member of the Ravens for less than a week, but he’s set to start at quarterback in the team’s game against the Chargers on Monday night.

The Ravens lost Lamar Jackson and Tyler Huntley to injuries in Week 8 and got the wheels moving on their trade for Brissett ahead of their loss to the Jaguars last Thursday night, but the deal didn’t become official until Friday. That left Brissett with a short window to pick up the offense for a 4-5 team that’s hoping to stay afloat in the second half of the season.

“It’s definitely been a crash course. No question,” Brissett said.

Brissett said it has “been a long, but fast, week” and said he’s confident in the work he’s done with the coaching staff to digest the offense. That work will continue — Brissett joked that the press conference was interrupting his crash course — and it will likely keep going until close to kickoff on Monday night.
