#2806. Harry Kane marks historic 126th England cap with a little reminder of his greatness

Harry Kane was asked this week to name the lowest moment of his 125 England caps, and his mind travelled back to that evening in Qatar when he sent a penalty over Hugo Lloris's crossbar and up to the heavens. Kane being Kane, he would score the next 30 penalties of his career. "It made me improve," he said. "A lot of the bad things have helped me get to where I am now."

Perhaps it was apt, then, that on the night of his record-breaking 126th England cap, Kane scored his 92nd England goal from the penalty spot to settle this 2-1 win over Croatia and book a spot in the Nations League quarter-finals. It was a little display of his work ethic, a simple kick of the ball honed to a fine point through thousands of repetitions on the training ground.

Kane dinked his penalty down the middle and a diving Dominik Livakovic almost blocked the ball with his trail leg. The goalkeeper beat the ground in frustration as if to say, I knew you'd do that. But perhaps this is the point: since that World Cup miss against France, Kane has trained every penalty into his armoury – the stutter or the run, the blast or the dink, left or right or the space a goalkeeper leaves behind – and Livakovic couldn't cover them all.

It came after a rare miss for Bayern Munich last month. "The next one is always important to bounce back," Kane said. "I did my usual routine yesterday in training and looked at the goalkeeper. I haven't gone down the middle with a penalty for maybe three or four years. With penalties, you have to keep things fresh, keep things moving. I thought it was a good time to do it, looking at his clips. Thankfully, it paid off today."

Kane's penalty evades the leg of Croatia goalkeeper Dominik Livakovic (PA)

What did we learn from this slightly underwhelming performance? England are better than Croatia, but then we saw that at the World Cup when England beat them 4-2, and more emphatically in Rijeka last month when England won 7-0. This was naturally a more cautious Croatia, against an altogether sloppier England who made a series of uncharacteristic errors all over the field.

Trent Alexander-Arnold gave the ball away on the edge of his own box, Lewis Hall played a fancy flick straight off the pitch and Jarell Quansah assisted Croatia's equaliser with a deft, daft header inside his own six-yard box. Jude Bellingham showed frustration towards some of his teammates' errors, yet his best game largely deserted him too.

The England fans who shivered through these 90 minutes might instead remember the few moments beforehand. The commemorations began with a giant Tifo behind one goal displaying England's captain roaring into the night sky with the words "England's finest" and "Kane 126" emblazoned top and bottom. Kane emerged from the tunnel with his four children as mascots, and then spent a couple of minutes waving around the Ballon d'Or before being presented with three gifts, like a scene from the nativity. The three wise men were played by Peter Shilton (gift one: a golden crest), Croatia captain Luka Modric (gift two: an old football), and Prince William (gift three: a framed shirt).

"It's been a crazy few weeks," Kane said. "It takes a lot out of you emotionally, physically and mentally ... it's a hard balance because you want to take it in. Breaking the record is a massive thing in the history of our game, seeing the tifo there, having my kids with me, and being presented by all the people was beyond dreams, so of course you have got to take it in. Once the whistle goes and you're on the pitch, you just start focusing on what your job is."

Kane poses with Prince Phillip and Peter Shilton (Reuters)

England started haphazardly but began to get a grip midway through the first half and it was Kane who unlocked Croatia's defence with a raking pass to Bellingham arriving at the far post. Bellingham's first-time cross found Morgan Rogers, who managed to impart just enough energy to the ball to bounce it home from three yards.

Kane had to wait. His first big chance came early in the second half when he received a cutback in the box from Declan Rice and a half-empty goal prepared to bulge, but Josip Stanisic wasn't feeling sentimental and he lunged in front to block Kane's goalbound shot.

Croatia drew level a few minutes later when Quansah took the extraordinary step of attempting a header back to Jordan Pickford inside a crowded penalty area. Pickford understandably wasn't expecting the pass, and he was beaten to the ball by Petar Sucic who knocked it into the roof of England's goal.

But Quansah went some way to making amends when his run drew some suspicious shirt pulling from Ivan Smolcic. Danish referee Morten Krogh missed it at first look but VAR intervened, and when Kane placed the ball down on the spot, the rest felt inevitable.

Kane celebrates after scoring from the spot (PA)

England now travel to Madrid to face Spain on Sunday, where victory would clinch top spot in the group. It would also serve as a sign of England's standing in the game right now if they were to beat the world champions on their own turf, after a narrow but encouraging 3-2 defeat by Spain at Wembley in September.

Kane will earn cap number 127 and he is intent on gathering many more. Since winning the Ballon d'Or he has made his remaining career targets abundantly clear: scoring 100 England goals, winning the Champions League with Bayern Munich and winning a major trophy for England.

Whether the Nations League counts as "major" is debatable – Cristiano Ronaldo might say yes but just about everyone else says no – yet it presents an opportunity to end England's 60-year wait for a men's trophy. It would be fitting if Kane, now England's greatest player by any measure, was the man to lift it.

#2805. NBA suspends Rockets G Marcus Smart 3 games for throwing punch at Warriors' Charles Bassey

The NBA doesn't look kindly on players who throw punches during a game. Houston Rockets guard Marcus Smart was suspended three games without pay by the NBA on Thursday after throwing a punch at Golden State Warriors center Charles Bassey during Wednesday's game.

In its statement, the league specifically says the punch made contact with Bassey's face. The suspension is set to cover Houston's games against the Utah Jazz on Friday, the Washington Wizards on Sunday and the Miami Heat on Monday.

The incident occurred in the third quarter of Houston's 115-108 win. With less than a minute to go in the quarter, Smart and Bassey appeared to exchange words on the court. Smart then threw a punch at Bassey right in front of an official.

Smart was assessed with a flagrant 2 foul and ejected from the contest. He had already exited the court by the time the officials announced the punishment.

Smart finished the game with 6 points, 2 rebounds and 1 assist.

Under NBA rules, Smart faced — at the minimum — a fine for the flagrant foul and a suspension for throwing a punch, which comes with a minimum one-game ban in the NBA rulebook:

Any player who throws a punch, whether it connects or not, has committed an unsportsmanlike act. He will be ejected following confirmation during review by instant replay and suspended for a minimum of one game.

The incident occurred after Smart and Bassey got tangled up during a rebound attempt. Following the contest, Rockets coach Ime Udoka said Smart confronted Bassey after the Warriors center wrapped up Smart's elbow on the play. Bassey reportedly told Smart "what are you gonna do," leading to the attempted punch.

Bassey, meanwhile, had something to say about what he called "fake toughness" from Smart:

"He's been playing dirty the whole game," Bassey said, according to Yahoo Sports' Kelly Iko. "I don't think he was trying to play, I think he was trying to get kicked out of the game."

Smart's absence didn't hurt the Rockets too badly in the end, as the team still managed to pull off a win. It probably helped that Warriors star Stephen Curry sustained an oblique tear early in the contest, missing much of the game.

Curry is now expected to miss some time with the oblique tear.

#2803. Patrick Mahomes on Josh Downs: You can tell the intellect, how he can pick things up

The Chiefs brought in a receiver at the trading deadline, acquiring Josh Downs from the Colts.

Quarterback Patrick Mahomes told reporters in his Wednesday press conference that he’s excited to get going with the wideout.

“I think you can tell just the intellect and how he’s able to pick stuff up and that he’s a professional, that he knows what he’s doing and he wants to come in and just compete and to have success, and he wants to win. And so, I’m excited for that,” Mahomes said, via transcript from the team. “And then you can just see him interacting with the other receivers in the room. You can tell that there’s going to be a great cohesion there, and that’s the type of guys you want on the team, that want to compete and that want to win.”

Mahomes noted he’s impressed with the way Downs can win his one-on-one matchups.

“He runs routes really well,” Mahomes said. “He sets them up really well where he will run a route early in the game that he sets up for another one later in the game. And then he has really good feel, you can tell, obviously, playing in the system that he has, he has a lot of read-type routes, and you can tell he knows how to get himself open, settle in the right zones, and win versus man [coverage] when he can.

“And so, I think it will be an easy transition for him to kind of do that stuff in our offense. Then, with how we do things, he can play a lot of different positions as we move receivers and tight ends around.”

The quarterback also sees it as realistic for Downs to play and contribute early on in his tenure with Kansas City. While that begins with plenty of reps at practice, Mahomes and the rest of the offense can direct Downs to where he needs to be during games if necessary.

“[W]e do a great job of kind of moving guys in and out of every single position, and so guys know the entire concepts of the plays,” Mahomes said. “We’re very detailed with how we call the plays in the huddle, which will help him out a ton.

“Then when he doesn’t know, being able to talk to me in the huddle or talk to the guys as they’re breaking the huddle, and that usually helps when the guys like each other. And so, it’s good to have a guy that you can tell everybody’s going to like, and that he has his head on the right way, and he’s going to make sure that he’s going to do whatever it takes to win.”

In nine games with the Colts this year, Downs caught 33 passes for 354 yards with one touchdown.

#2802. Defense must show for Bengals to win AFC North showdown with Steelers

There's no getting around it: This edition of the Cincinnati Bengals versus Pittsburgh Steelers is a truly big game.

It's November, it's chilly and getting colder, and the top spot in the AFC North will be on the line Nov. 15 when the Bengals and Steelers meet at Paycor Stadium for an 8:20 p.m. Sunday Night Football game. It's cliche, but this is what it's all about.

Based on what the teams have on their respective schedules in the months to come, the winner of the Nov. 15 clash could have a significant leg-up in determining the AFC North champion. It's a must-win for both clubs no matter how you look at it.

Bengals trade deadline Zac Taylor Bengals take accountability for failed trade attempt with Eagles

Here are some considerations and must-haves for the Bengals if they're to emerge atop the AFC North following this game:

Sure, Cincinnati would love to have its defense create chaos for 60 minutes against the Steelers. Negative-yardage plays. Turnovers. Making Steelers quarterback Aaron Rodgers uncomfortable all night. The Bengals are more than welcome to do all of that against the Steelers − but it won't take 60 minutes of perfection to get the win Cincinnati needs.

The Bengals defense has its issues. Missed tackles and a tendency to allow explosive plays are chief among them. Teams have racked up big yardage totals against the Bengals, too. But the bottom line is what opponents convert into actual points, and Cincinnati's 21.9 opponent points per game, which ranks No. 14 in the NFL, is solid.

Cincinnati has held opposing offenses to 20 points or less three times this season, and that sets the team's explosive offense up nicely to out-gun almost any team in the NFL. And bear in mind that while the Steelers are one of two teams to put up 30 points on the defense this year, Pittsburgh is far from the most prolific offense in the league. Give Pittsburgh full marks for its 6-2 start, but the offense's 22.9 points per game is ranked just the No. 17 unit in the NFL. That's a number the Bengals offense can more than keep up with if the defense can set the table.

If the Bengals' defense can just get off the field in key spots and generally be what it's been all season, that'll be "mission accomplished" for coordinator Al Golden's unit.

When divisional opponents meet for the second time in an NFL season, those games are often framed within the context of the last time the teams met. Sometimes, that's fair to do, but not in this case. If you're the Bengals, keep these facts in mind about the Sept. 27 loss at Acrisure Stadium because a lot has changed in the 49 days between that game and Nov. 15

The Bengals' offensive line is playing at a higher level than it was Sept. 27. It's been a while since quarterback Joe Burrow was getting knocked around like he was in September (and don't forget the Steelers clinched their Sept. 27 win with a sack and a fumble recovery late in regulation).

Burrow has grown into the 2026 season and is now playing some of the best football of his career. Back in Week 3, the talk around Burrow was about a slower start to his year than expected. Today? He's leading the NFL with 23 touchdown passes and a 74% completion percentage, and is No. 2 in passing yards with 2,386 yards.

Since the Bengals are coming off a win in Madrid, there's also been considerable talk about the 0-5 record of NFL teams coming off an international game when playing a team coming off a bye. And the stat makes sense: There's no question about which team would be more rested, but these matchups are a relatively new thing (hence there are only five prior examples). In the past, playing abroad meant going straight into your bye, but that appears to be a bygone luxury for teams and schedule-makers. For the Bengals, it's not quantum physics or higher math, though. They can figure out how to do this and become the first team in this situation to win coming off playing abroad. The team has eased back into preparations for Pittsburgh with great care. For instance, Wednesday's practice inside Paycor Stadium was a walk-through as players continued to reacclimatize.

Basically, the Sept. 27 game isn't so relevant this week. Neither is that 0-5 record, which has nothing whatsoever to do with the Bengals. These subjects are just noise, and Cincinnati needs to block them out.

The 2026 Bengals were invested in heavily by the front office for games exactly like this one. Dexter Lawrence II, Boye Mafe, Jonathan Allen? And don't forget the Burrow-Ja'Marr Chase-Tee Higgins triumvirate. All of these players (and their price tags) were assembled to win these high-stakes games.

Since the spring, all the talk has been about the Bengals contending for an AFC North title, and more. Well, that means this team's stars have to show up, lead, and deliver. Not next week against the Commanders, and certainly in two weeks when the national TV cameras are far from Paycor Stadium when the New Orleans Saints come to town.

Based on the number of remaining games, the math suggests the Bengals could go 0-2 against Pittsburgh and still accomplish all their goals. Reality, and the makeup of those remaining games, indicates there's far less math to suggest Cincinnati can win the North by going winless against the Steelers.

It's been years since Cincinnati had a game like this in its backyard. Burrow lobbied for the pieces that could allow the organization to be successful on nights like this. It's time to go.

#2801. Tokenization platform Securitize gains 10% postmarket on Q3 results, move into asset-backed finance

Tokenization platform Securitize climbed about 10% postmarket after reporting third quarter growth alongside increased operating expenses, and announcing an expansion into asset-backed finance through a strategic acquisition.

Miami-based Securitize, which made its public market debut in July, operates in the relatively new corner of the crypto equity universe involved in institutional tokenization infrastructure. Tokenized assets on the platform grew 9% in the third quarter, to $4.8 billion, from the same period a year ago. Aggregate transaction volume rose 20% from a year ago, to $4.3 billion. Revenue of $11.5 million was up 28% from last year.

The growth came as bullish sentiment around tokenization gained traction throughout the third quarter, ahead of the Security and Exchange Commission formalizing the long awaited Innovation Exemption on Sept. 17, which created a temporary pathway for trading of certain tokenized stocks and strengthened the regulatory outlook.

Profitability is still meaningfully below year-ago levels, due in part to costs associated with going public over the summer, as well as the scaling up of the tokenization business at an active time for the entire sector, Securitize CEO Carlos Domingo told CNBC in an exclusive interview.

Securitize reinforced "certain areas of the company like finance, compliance, legal [and] cybersecurity," which increased corporate costs "compared to a year ago when we were privately held," Domingo said. "We're in in a very good position. The adjusted EBITDA … is improving significantly from previous quarters."

Securitize reported an adjusted EBITDA loss of $2 million, compared with positive adjusted EBITDA of $3.6 million a year ago, better than a $5.5 million loss in the second quarter. The company posted a 183% increase in operating costs and expenses.

Also on Monday, the Securitize board authorized a $50 million share buyback and announced the acquisition of River One, an institutional asset-backed financing platform developing technology to connect stablecoin capital with traditional lending markets. Terms were not disclosed.

Securitize sees an opportunity to modernize the $9 trillion market for asset backed finance by using stablecoins to finance real world assets, generate yield and tokenize investment positions and credit products, Domingo said.The acquisition also marks an expansion beyond Securitize's core niche markets at a time when tokenization's competitive landscape is growing, with traditional asset managers like BlackRock and Franklin Templeton, and established financial market infrastructure providers like DTCC and Broadridge, entering the industry.

"We have been very active in tokenized funds, treasuries [and] yield … And we've been obviously recently very active in tokenized stocks, so this adds another piece of the puzzle in the emerging tokenization space for us to capture revenue growth."

#2797. AFC North Week 9 Recap: Joe Burrow strengthens MVP bid

The AFC North standings did not change in Week 9, with only one team getting a win.

The Steelers enjoyed their bye week at the top of the division. Pittsburgh will now have to defend first place when they return to action in Week 10 against the Bengals.

Cincinnati moved to 6-2 with a 34-17 win over the Atlanta Falcons in Madrid, Spain.

Joe Burrow has firmly planted himself in the MVP race following another strong performance. Against the Falcons, Burrow completed 24 of 29 passes for 271 yards and three touchdowns while adding another touchdown on the ground. Over his last four games, Burrow has accounted for 18 touchdowns. Ja'Marr Chase was Burrow's leading receiver against Atlanta with 10 catches for 155 yards and a touchdown, while Tee Higgins had a pair of touchdowns to go with 32 yards on five catches.

The Bengals defense did not record a sack on quarterback Michael Penix, but the unit did enough to hold the Falcons' talented offense in check. Barrett Carter had the lone turnover of the game with an interception off Penix. Cincinnati will have the opportunity to challenge for the division lead now in Week 10.

The Browns dropped their second consecutive game with a 23-22 loss to the New Orleans Saints.

Shedeur Sanders got the start at quarterback in place of the injured Deshaun Watson in Week 9. Sanders finished the game 19 of 31 for 297 yards and two touchdowns, with another 28 yards on 10 carries. Denzel Boston's 98 receiving yards on six catches led the offense, while KC Concepcion and Harold Fannin Jr. were the recipients of Sanders' touchdowns. Fannin's touchdown was particularly impressive as he broke multiple tackles on his way to a 39-yard score in overtime. Cleveland went for the win with a two-point attempt, but Sanders was unable to connect with Boston on the play.

The Browns' defense racked up five sacks on Tyler Shough, with Carson Schwesinger and Myles Harden both finishing with two. Schwesinger had 15 total tackles while also forcing a fumble to go with his pair of sacks.

The Ravens lost their fourth game in a row with a 33-10 loss to the Jacksonville Jaguars on Thursday Night Football.

Undrafted rookie Joe Fagnano started the game at quarterback on a short week after both Lamar Jackson and Tyler Huntley were injured in the previous game. Fagnano struggled as you would expect, completing 10 of 18 pass attempts for just 60 yards and a pair of interceptions. Justice Hill was the leading rusher with nine carries for 85 yards and a touchdown, with most of the work coming on a 65-yard score in the fourth quarter. Baltimore has since acquired veteran quarterback Jacoby Brissett from the Arizona Cardinals, whom they hope can keep their season alive for a potential Jackson return down the road.

The Ravens' defense had a tough hill to climb with little to no help from their own offense, but the unit was supposed to be a strength of the team heading into the season. Instead, Baltimore's defense has allowed over 30 points in three straight games.

#2796. Defense must show for Bengals to win AFC North showdown with Steelers

There's no getting around it: This edition of the Cincinnati Bengals versus Pittsburgh Steelers is a truly big game.

It's November, it's chilly and getting colder, and the top spot in the AFC North will be on the line Nov. 15 when the Bengals and Steelers meet at Paycor Stadium for an 8:20 p.m. Sunday Night Football game. It's cliche, but this is what it's all about.

Based on what the teams have on their respective schedules in the months to come, the winner of the Nov. 15 clash could have a significant leg-up in determining the AFC North champion. It's a must-win for both clubs no matter how you look at it.

Bengals trade deadline Zac Taylor Bengals take accountability for failed trade attempt with Eagles

Here are some considerations and must-haves for the Bengals if they're to emerge atop the AFC North following this game:

Sure, Cincinnati would love to have its defense create chaos for 60 minutes against the Steelers. Negative-yardage plays. Turnovers. Making Steelers quarterback Aaron Rodgers uncomfortable all night. The Bengals are more than welcome to do all of that against the Steelers − but it won't take 60 minutes of perfection to get the win Cincinnati needs.

The Bengals defense has its issues. Missed tackles and a tendency to allow explosive plays are chief among them. Teams have racked up big yardage totals against the Bengals, too. But the bottom line is what opponents convert into actual points, and Cincinnati's 21.9 opponent points per game, which ranks No. 14 in the NFL, is solid.

Cincinnati has held opposing offenses to 20 points or less three times this season, and that sets the team's explosive offense up nicely to out-gun almost any team in the NFL. And bear in mind that while the Steelers are one of two teams to put up 30 points on the defense this year, Pittsburgh is far from the most prolific offense in the league. Give Pittsburgh full marks for its 6-2 start, but the offense's 22.9 points per game is ranked just the No. 17 unit in the NFL. That's a number the Bengals offense can more than keep up with if the defense can set the table.

If the Bengals' defense can just get off the field in key spots and generally be what it's been all season, that'll be "mission accomplished" for coordinator Al Golden's unit.

When divisional opponents meet for the second time in an NFL season, those games are often framed within the context of the last time the teams met. Sometimes, that's fair to do, but not in this case. If you're the Bengals, keep these facts in mind about the Sept. 27 loss at Acrisure Stadium because a lot has changed in the 49 days between that game and Nov. 15

The Bengals' offensive line is playing at a higher level than it was Sept. 27. It's been a while since quarterback Joe Burrow was getting knocked around like he was in September (and don't forget the Steelers clinched their Sept. 27 win with a sack and a fumble recovery late in regulation).

Burrow has grown into the 2026 season and is now playing some of the best football of his career. Back in Week 3, the talk around Burrow was about a slower start to his year than expected. Today? He's leading the NFL with 23 touchdown passes and a 74% completion percentage, and is No. 2 in passing yards with 2,386 yards.

Since the Bengals are coming off a win in Madrid, there's also been considerable talk about the 0-5 record of NFL teams coming off an international game when playing a team coming off a bye. And the stat makes sense: There's no question about which team would be more rested, but these matchups are a relatively new thing (hence there are only five prior examples). In the past, playing abroad meant going straight into your bye, but that appears to be a bygone luxury for teams and schedule-makers. For the Bengals, it's not quantum physics or higher math, though. They can figure out how to do this and become the first team in this situation to win coming off playing abroad. The team has eased back into preparations for Pittsburgh with great care. For instance, Wednesday's practice inside Paycor Stadium was a walk-through as players continued to reacclimatize.

Basically, the Sept. 27 game isn't so relevant this week. Neither is that 0-5 record, which has nothing whatsoever to do with the Bengals. These subjects are just noise, and Cincinnati needs to block them out.

The 2026 Bengals were invested in heavily by the front office for games exactly like this one. Dexter Lawrence II, Boye Mafe, Jonathan Allen? And don't forget the Burrow-Ja'Marr Chase-Tee Higgins triumvirate. All of these players (and their price tags) were assembled to win these high-stakes games.

Since the spring, all the talk has been about the Bengals contending for an AFC North title, and more. Well, that means this team's stars have to show up, lead, and deliver. Not next week against the Commanders, and certainly in two weeks when the national TV cameras are far from Paycor Stadium when the New Orleans Saints come to town.

Based on the number of remaining games, the math suggests the Bengals could go 0-2 against Pittsburgh and still accomplish all their goals. Reality, and the makeup of those remaining games, indicates there's far less math to suggest Cincinnati can win the North by going winless against the Steelers.

It's been years since Cincinnati had a game like this in its backyard. Burrow lobbied for the pieces that could allow the organization to be successful on nights like this. It's time to go.

#2794. Jim Harbaugh: Broncos took advantage of a loophole the NFL should not allow

Chargers head coach Jim Harbaugh is not happy with the way the Broncos backed out of a trade for Chargers linebacker Daiyan Henley, or with the NFL rules that allowed them to do so.

After agreeing to trade a fourth-round pick and a fifth-round pick for Henley, the Broncos told Chargers GM Joe Hortiz they couldn’t go through with it because Henley’s physical revealed concerns about his knee. The Broncos were still willing to trade for Henley, but wanted to give up less. Harbaugh believes physical exams should only be to determine whether or not the player is healthy, not to renegotiate trade terms.

“What happened with the trade? Here’s what I know, from talking to Joe: The Broncos based a pass-fail on a medical grade based on whether we would take less compensation, and we wouldn’t and we didn’t,” Harbaugh said. “In my opinion it’s one of those loopholes that the league should really look into, because it sets a bad precedent.”

Asked if it’s permitted for teams to renegotiate trades after the trade deadline, Harbaugh answered, “It shouldn’t be, but apparently it is. That’s why it’s a loophole, and teams should not have the ability to do that.”

Harbaugh said the Broncos offered to say Henley passed his physical, but only if the Chargers would take something less than the fifth-round pick the Broncos had agreed to.

“It’s a medical grade. It’s a pass-fail for a medical grade, and they told us they weren’t going to pass him if it’s a fourth and a fifth, which was agreed upon — they would not pass him for that. They would pass him for a fourth and a fill in the blank. I don’t know what to call that, but that’s what it is,” Harbaugh said.

Harbaugh is right that the NFL has set a bad precedent. By allowing teams to renegotiate trade terms after the deadline, the NFL is incentivizing teams to engage in chicanery on physical exams to squeeze their trade partners into taking less favorable deals. The Chargers refused to budge, but the NFL rules shouldn’t have put the Chargers in a position where they had to make that decision.

#2792. JJ Redick on why he reinserted Luka Doncic late in Wednesday's game

For the Los Angeles Lakers, Luka Doncic normally rests during the first several minutes of the second and fourth quarters of games. When they entered the fourth quarter of Wednesday's game against the Milwaukee Bucks, they held a 94-73 lead, and with 6:51 remaining, they were still up by 20 points.

That is when Luka Doncic came back into the game, which was surely something that had fans wondering whether it was a wise decision. Sure enough, a little later, he landed on Bucks forward Kyle Kuzma while making a long jumper and tweaked his right ankle, forcing him to exit the game almost immediately.

Typically, coaches are reluctant to put their stars and starters back in a game deep into the fourth quarter when their team has a big lead in order to avoid injuries. Lakers coach JJ Redick was asked after Wednesday's game why he reinserted Doncic despite being in full control, via Lakers Nation.

"We decided, because of how that Raptors game went, we said alright, four minutes, 20 points, somewhere around there that's kind of the cutoff point. I don't think Luka would have gotten back in the game had Collin [Sexton] not busted his head open. So not having Collin, not having AR (Austin Reaves), you put Luka back in, you're playing for two minutes. He's gonna either keep the lead or build the lead and then he tweaks his ankle. There's nothing you can do about that."

Reaves, of course, had to leave the game in the third quarter due to a heel ailment he sustained on a dunk. Then midway through the fourth quarter, Sexton suffered an open wound on his head after a head-to-head collision with Bucks guard Ryan Rollins. Sexton required sutures to close the resulting wound, according to Redick.

The good news is that Doncic may be OK for the Lakers' next game, which won't be until Sunday and will take place against the Phoenix Suns. Shams Charania of ESPN reported on Thursday that Doncic woke up without pain in the right ankle he tweaked.

Even without Doncic, Reaves and Sexton, the Lakers finished off the Bucks for a 123-112 victory that gave them an 11-2 record and a seven-game winning streak. That 11-2 mark is the best record in the NBA.

#2788. Warburton out to impress the world against Adames

Welsh boxer Gerome Warburton says facing world champion Carlos Adames will complete his boxing journey as he looks to prove he belongs at the top level.

The 31-year-old will face WBC middleweight champion Adames, 32, in Riyadh, Saudi Arabia, on Saturday, 14 November, though the world title is not on the line.

The WBA continental champion is not ranked by the WBC, and will instead face once-beaten Adames at super-middleweight in a non-title main event.

Warburton, from Colwyn Bay, believes he has earned this career-defining shot after years of overcoming the odds on the British scene.

"You're in there to do a job. Yes, he's a world champion, but this is my time to show the world that I belong at this level," Warburton told BBC Sport.

"All of my hard work has got me here. This is a big hurdle to get over, but I've been jumping over hurdles my whole career."

The 5ft 11in (1.8m) former Welsh middleweight champion added: "This is another occasion to prove wrong those people who think I can't achieve something. I don't feel any pressure - the pressure is all on him.

"He's the WBC champion. He's the one who has set out to put on a show-stopping performance as world champ. I'm going there to upset the apple cart."

Workaholic with a tattooed throat – meet The Merthyr Mexican

Price rules out Shields but hints at big showdown

Headlining Zuffa Boxing's first show in Saudi Arabia will mark a new high point in a "crazy" rise for Warburton, who never set out to become a world champion.

He faces a quick turnaround, too, having beaten veteran Englishman Nathan Heaney to retain his WBA continental middleweight title on 26 September.

Consecutive victories over former British champion Heaney have given Warburton's career fresh momentum, exceeding even his own expectations.

"I never thought that beating Nathan Heaney twice would have gotten me where I am now. I took the risk to face him twice, and it's brought a reward," Warburton said.

Initially, Warburton had his eyes on the Anthony Joshua vs Tyson Fury undercard in Cardiff before landing a fight that he thought was a joke when first informed.

"I was back in the gym training full time and had a phone call from my manager telling me they had asked Zuffa if I could face a certain boxer," Warburton recalled.

"They came back and said no, but that I could face Carlos Adames. I was taken aback and thought, 'Are you telling me lies? Is this true?'

"After a split second, it hit me that I had finally got a good reward for all of my hard work, and then he dropped the news that it wouldn't be for his WBC title.

"As they say in boxing, things happen quickly. Beat Carlos Adames and I could be on the road for the WBC world title."

Gerome Warburton beat Nathan Heaney for the WBA continental middleweight title in March [Getty Images]

Wales has a rich history of world champion boxers, but none of the 14 men and women who have claimed top honours in the sport hail from north Wales.

Barmouth-born Johnny Williams held the British and Empire heavyweight titles, but he received his boxing education after moving to English town Rugby at age three.

Warburton now has the chance to become north Wales' most celebrated boxer, as he prepares to become the first from the region to face a world champion.

"I feel as though I don't get the recognition for doing that," he added.

"I'm holding the flag for north Wales. If I could get my hands on a world title, or box for one, it would be a dream come true, and I'd be writing history."

Warburton, who goes by the moniker 'The Breadmaker', will not be joined by family for the trip to Saudi Arabia, but knows they will be watching back home.

While son Roman was present for both fights against Heaney and celebrated in the ring, Warburton prefers to "zone in" when fighting further afield.

"Unfortunately, I've banned all of my family members from going over because I treat boxing like work," Warburton explained.

"My family come when I fight closer to home, but when I'm away, I like to be on my own with my team. They wouldn't come to work with me.

"I have to be zoned in, and I know that everyone will be watching from home. One of my friends is coming because he likes to be adventurous."

Warburton expects the Riyadh crowd to be behind Adames and believes that will play into his hands, as it has done throughout his career.

"I'm used to not having the crowd [behind me] and having them boo me. The two Heaney fights showed that," he said.

"I could go into the lion's den, and it still wouldn't bother me."

Beyond the boxing accolades, Warburton knows how much an upset victory over Adames would change his family's life.

"Win this and everything changes for my family, and I would probably become the mandatory challenger," he said.

"I've got someone in front of me who is at the highest level. To even be in the ring with him means I've completed boxing in a way, regardless of what happens.

"I'm a lad from north Wales who only had an ambition of winning a Welsh title. To step in with a world champion is what dreams are made of."

#2784. Mike Brown not ready to make a Mikal Bridges role change despite offensive struggles

Mikal Bridges is basically a starter in name only at the moment.

His minutes and production both resemble a bench player so far this season. It's really only his inclusion in the pregame introductions that represents his status as a starter.

But Knicks coach Mike Brown is not yet ready to make a change.

"Mikal is a starter, so it's not a question of that," Brown said. "Like a lot of players, they've had their good moments and their not-so-good moments. Mikal has a proven track record of playing at a pretty high level as a starter in this league. So for me to make a quote-unquote panic decision and change it, right now we're [11] games in or whatever we are, I'm not feeling it."

New York Knicks guard Mikal Bridges (25) reaches for the ball during the first quarter against the Cleveland Cavaliers, Wednesday, Nov. 11, 2026, in New York. Charles Wenzelberg / New York Post

Bridges played just 17 minutes in the Knicks' 115-107 loss to the Cavs Wednesday night at Madison Square Garden and only recorded two points. He was on the bench for the entirety of the fourth quarter.

For the season, Bridges is averaging 24.4 minutes per game — notably fewer than Landry Shamet's 25.9 minutes per game despite Shamet coming off the bench eight of the Knicks' 11 games.

Bridges' offensive struggles to start the year have resulted in Brown increasingly opting to go with Shamet or Miles McBride on the court, particularly to close games. Shamet and McBride have played 69 and 43 fourth-quarter minutes this year, respectively, compared to Bridges' 41. Bridges' 5.9 points per game are eighth most on the team, and he is shooting a woeful 32.9 percent from the field and 26.5 percent from 3-point range.

He has scored just four total points in fourth quarters this year — fewer than bench-warmers Pacôme Dadiet and James Wiseman, who usually only play in garbage time, have produced. It's half of what Dalton Knecht, who has played in just three games this year, has delivered in fourth quarters.

Brown's changing sentiment is telling. He began the year saying that his starting five was entrenched and that everyone else on the roster was fighting for minutes. Then, last week, he said that Shamet is "about as close to being a starter as you can imagine for us." And after Wednesday's game, Brown admitted that he feels a need to make room for Shamet and McBride.

New York Knicks head coach Mike Brown reacts on the baseline during the second quarter of an NBA game against the Milwaukee Bucks on Tuesday, Nov. 10, 2026. Charles Wenzelberg / New York Post

Bridges can usually at least hang his hat on the defensive end, but that's an area in which Shamet and McBride are thriving as well. Brown has praised their point-of-attack defense and said that their tracking data has shown that Shamet has been their best pick-and-roll defender this season.

"We got two guys that are playing really well in Landry and Deuce," Brown said. "So it's hard. It's gonna be hard to find minutes at times for Mikal. And maybe even at times for Josh [Hart], because those two guys are playing at a pretty high level. Not just on the offensive end of the floor, but defensively too. … Landry and Deuce have been a big part of what we've done defensively. They're playing at a high level. I gotta keep trying to find ways to get those guys on the floor."

The Knicks have certainly been here before with Bridges. He went through significant slumps each of the past two regular seasons before coming up big in the postseason.

Last year, it reached a crescendo during the first round of the playoffs, when the Knicks fell to a 2-1 series deficit to the Hawks. Brown repeatedly sat Bridges in the second halves of those first three games and faced constant questions about replacing him in the starting lineup.

But Brown held firm with Bridges, who rewarded him by playing a key role in the championship run.

The Knicks are confident a similar bounce back is looming.

"No matter what's going on, with what people are saying or anything, the people in this room have his back night in and night out," Jalen Brunson said. "We see the work he puts into his game. We see the time that he puts in. When you're doing the right things day in and day out, things will eventually fall in your favor. So just giving him support, giving him confidence every single day, regardless of what the noise is on the outside. That's our brother, we're gonna keep it that way."

#2781. Chiefs Predicted to Add Tyreek Hill on ‘Bargain’ Deal to Help Patrick Mahomes

The Kansas City Chiefs made one big move to help their wide receiving corps at the NFL trade deadline and fell short on another, but could still have time to add more help for Patrick Mahomes.

The Chiefs swung a deal with the Indianapolis Colts, taking advantage of the team's fire sale to land steady receiver Josh Downs. The team reportedly took an even bigger swing, reaching out to the New York Jets about star receiver Garrett Wilson, but were denied.

Bleacher Report's Moe Moton identified another potential addition for the Chiefs, suggesting they could still reunite with Tyreek Hill on a "bargain" deal.

Moton noted that there are still plenty of questions surrounding Hill, from the condition of his knee after last season's major injury to the result of a civil trial from his estranged wife where he was found liable on two charges of battery.

"Hill's injury recovery and the civil jury's findings could factor into teams' evaluations, though a team willing to sign him may still see potential for the explosiveness that made him a game-changing receiver," Moton wrote.

Moton suggested that Hill wouldn't be close to a return until after Thanksgiving, when the Chiefs might be able to add him for the final stretch of the season.

"Even with the addition of wideout Josh Downs, the Chiefs may consider reuniting with Hill to add another playmaker for Patrick Mahomes," Moton wrote.

Hill's agent, Drew Rosenhaus, said this week in an appearance on the "Pat McAfee Show" that the wide receiver is close to being medically cleared to return. Rosenhaus didn't give an exact timeframe of when this could happen.

"As soon as Tyreek Hill is healthy and cleared medically I'm letting teams know they can sign him," Rosenhaus said. "I think he's close and I think he's gonna make a great comeback..I expect him to be playing and making things happen this season."

In the meantime, the Chiefs are ready to get Downs into the mix quickly. Mahomes said this week that the wide receiver has gone to work immediately learning the offense.

"You can tell just the intellect and how he's able to pick stuff up. He's a professional," Mahomes said, via NFL.com. "He knows what he's doing, and he wants to come in and just compete and have success."

Mahomes added that the Chiefs and Colts have similar offensive concepts, which helps, but said it will still take time for the veteran receiver to get fully up to speed.

"The entire offense, it'll probably take a couple weeks (to learn)," Mahomes said. "But I think what I've learned just from meeting Josh is he's very intelligent."

#2778. Warriors’ center gets last laugh as NBA suspends Rockets star for several games

Warriors center Charles Bassey kept his hands down when Marcus Smart swung at him.

The Houston Rockets guard will now sit for three games after the NBA suspended him Thursday for throwing a punch that made contact with the Golden State Warriors center's face.

The incident came with 21 seconds left in the third quarter of Houston's 115-108 win over Golden State. Smart and Bassey got tangled up during a possession, then continued jawing as they ran back up the floor.

Golden State Warriors center Charles Bassey, left, and Houston Rockets guard Marcus Smart, center, react during an NBA game in Houston, Nov. 11, 2026. AP Photo/Ashley Landis

Smart turned and threw a punch that grazed Bassey's nose. Officials assessed a Flagrant 2, and Smart walked off before they announced the ejection.

Bassey said afterward that Smart had been playing dirty throughout the game and dismissed the punch as "fake toughness."

Rockets coach Ime Udoka offered Smart's version: the guard said Bassey had bent an elbow he had injured earlier in the season. Udoka said Bassey then challenged Smart, who responded with the swing.

Golden State Warriors center Charles Bassey, center left, is held back after a confrontation with Houston Rockets guard Marcus Smart, center, Wednesday, Nov. 11, 2026. AP Photo/Ashley Landis

The league's decision draws a line under the altercation, but it won't change the result for Golden State. Bassey did not retaliate, and the confrontation ended without a larger fight or additional ejections.

Smart will miss Houston's games against the Utah Jazz on Friday, the Washington Wizards on Sunday and the Miami Heat on Monday. He is averaging 8.3 points, 2.6 rebounds and two assists in nine games as a reserve for the Rockets.

Brandin Podziemski #2 of the Golden State Warriors drives to the basket against Marcus Smart #36 of the Houston Rockets on Wednesday, Nov. 11, 2026. Getty Images

It was already a costly night for the Warriors: Stephen Curry left in the first quarter with right abdominal soreness, and Golden State's eight-game winning streak ended with the defeat.

Bassey's composure kept the incident from compounding the loss in the moment; Smart's suspension means he will watch Houston's next three games from the sidelines.

#2776. Knoxville, Chattanooga Fox affiliates pass on Titans game, for now

The Tennessee Titans apparently need an asterisk on their name.

At least two TV markets in the state for which the team is named have passed on televising their Week 10 game against the Jaguars.

Drew Lerner of Awful Announcing explains that 506sports.com coverage map shows that the Knoxville and Chattanooga Fox affiliates will not televise the Jaguars-Titans game. Instead, Vikings-Packers will be shown in the 1:00 p.m. ET window.

It’s a preliminary map, as Lerner notes. The Fox affiliates in Knoxville and Chattanooga, the third- and fourth-largest cities in the league by way of population, could change their minds.

Making the situation even more unusual is the fact that Fox won’t get many Titans games, because they’re usually on CBS. The obvious takeaway is that the Fox affiliates in Knoxville and Chattanooga believe that more people will watch Packers-Vikings than will watch Jaguars-Titans.

It’s not personal, it’s business. And the affiliates think they’re business interests are best served by showing Packers-Vikings not Jaguars-Titans.

#2774. Has Ravens’ Zay Flowers talked to Lamar Jackson about potential 2026 return?

Has Ravens' Zay Flowers talked to Lamar Jackson about potential 2026 return? appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Zay Flowers provided an update on his latest interactions with Lamar Jackson as the Baltimore Ravens gear up for the second half of the 2026 NFL season.

Flowers is going through the fourth season of his career with the Ravens. He shines as one of the best wideouts in the league, often having his best performances with Jackson leading the offense at quarterback.

However, Jackson is down for the count as he is on injured reserve with a significant thumb injury. This prompted Baltimore to acquire Jacoby Brissett from the Arizona Cardinals, assisting Flowers and the offense moving forward.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

Flowers spoke with media on Thursday, per reporter Sam Cohn. He admitted he hasn't talked to Jackson recently, preferring to let the quarterback make a full recovery without interruptions.

"Nah, I haven't talked to Lamar about [coming back]. I'm just letting him rest," Flowers said.

Zay Flowers and the Ravens still have a chance to get back into the playoff mix. However, it will require them to win most of their games while needing teams above them to sustain more losses.

Flowers has delivered solid performances throughout the first half of the season. After eight games, he made 43 catches for 614 yards and three touchdowns. He looks forward to seeing his production stabilize with Jacoby Brissett starting at quarterback.

Baltimore has a 4-5 record this season, sitting at the bottom of the AFC North Division standings. They trail the Cleveland Browns, the Cincinnati Bengals and the Pittsburgh Steelers at the moment. In terms of the conference standings, they are 10th place. They are above the Houston Texans and the New York Jets while being under the Las Vegas Raiders and the Browns.

The Ravens will continue preparations for their next matchup, being at home. They host the Los Angeles Chargers in Week 10 as kickoff commences on Nov. 16 at 8:15 p.m. ET.

#2772. LeBron James out, Joel Embiid available; Adem Bona seeing a specialist

Update, 6:30 p.m.: Joel Embiid has been upgraded to available.

Update, 1 p.m.: LeBron James has officially been ruled out with low back/sciatica management.

The Toronto Raptors have ruled out Scottie Barnes (left groin strain) and Trayce Jackson-Davis (illness). Kawhi Leonard remains out with a left knee injury.

As the Philadelphia 76ers gear up for tonight's home game against the Toronto Raptors, we received a good sign on the availability of two of their stars. Joel Embiid and LeBron James both participated in morning shootaround after being placed on the injury report as questionable yesterday evening. Embiid even spoke to reporters, but could not provide any further information about whether he will suit up tonight.

Nevertheless, you'd rather see the two of them out there than not. The Sixers could certainly use their two largest starters against Toronto, particularly with the handful of absences already in the frontcourt.

On that note, Dominick Barlow also participated in shootaround, but there is still no timeline for his return. Barlow has yet to make his season debut due to back soreness, but does appear to be getting closer. On the less optimistic side of things, though, backup center Adem Bona was said to have seen a specialist and will be out at least a week before being re-evaluated. He last played on Nov. 4 in Detroit, sitting out recent games due to a cervical spine sprain. Without Bona, the Sixers have been playing small in the minutes when Embiid sits. Nick Nurse has not opted to utilize two-way contract big man Tacko Fall, who has only appeared in three minutes of garbage time this season.

We'll await word closer to game time on the availability of Embiid and James. Either way, expect plenty of minutes for Dean Wade and Jabari Walker. Stay tuned.
